A mail for every change to how a person signs in (sign-in and access, area 3: A8, E1).

- **New email template: `securityNotice`** (`capsomer/email`). It takes `title`, `what`, `when` and `secure`, with optional `device`, `location` and `url`, the fields of site-runtime 0.45.0's `sendSecurityNotice` `detail`, so a site renders the notice with `renderEmail(securityNotice, { brand, to: user.email, ...detail, url })`. The mail says what changed and when, then "Device:" and "Near:" lines when it has them, what to do if it was not the person, a "Review your account" button with `url`, and that it holds no link that signs in. The subject is "{brand}: {title}". It takes `copy` like the others. States page: two new frames. No class, token or HTML contract changes.
- **Site only: the report endpoint is rate limited and connect-src names one file** (no package change). `/csp-report` passes site-runtime's receiver an `allow` backed by a Workers rate-limit binding (`CSP_REPORT_RATE_LIMIT` in `wrangler.jsonc`, 60 a minute per address), so a flood no longer reaches the log. The site's `connect-src` names the results file it reads (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DrDustinEdwards/capsomer/results/results.json`) instead of every repository on raw.githubusercontent.com. `test/unit/security-headers.test.mjs` is removed: the family's live header check grades the public site.
- **Site only: static files carry their charset and cross-origin CORP where another origin loads them** (no package change). The site's `_headers` gives every static text file `charset=utf-8`, and stylesheets, scripts and fonts `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin` (site-runtime 0.43.0 `headersFile({ charset: true })` and `corpFor`).
- **Showcase site: each page's WebPage and breadcrumb.** `site/seo.ts` builds heads with Prelum's `defineHead`, so the theme builder and every component page carry a WebPage and a Home > page BreadcrumbList, and the canonical keeps the slash the pages are served at.
- The posts list names site-api v0.17.0, the release its Trash, rename, preview links, authors and excerpts shipped in: site-api 0.16.0 went to the 404 report first. Text only; no HTML contract changes.

## 0.53.0

Charts move to Enarratio 0.4 (the charts checklist, area 10, 2026-10-31). Enarratio now draws the whole figure, so the chart frame is a thin layout around it instead of a second figure. Needs Enarratio 0.4.0.

- **Chart palette: every pair 10 CIEDE2000 apart under every color vision.** The gate (`SERIES_MIN_DE`, `tokens/chart-checks.mjs`) rises from 8 to 10, Enarratio 0.4's bar below which `stylesheet()` throws. Series 1 to 3 stay purple, fox and teal; the companions are retuned, the same in every family. Light: `--series-4` `#749455` to `#709161`, `--series-5` `#505ca1` to `#51579a`, `--series-6` `#046d5e` to `#016a5c`, `--series-7` `#c564a1` to `#c06caa`, `--series-8` `#98445b` to `#9d412a`. Dark: `#a3c584` to `#abce9b`, `#a4b5ff` to `#a6b0fb`, `#65cbb6` to `#59cdb7`, `#c3629f` to `#bf6ba9`, `#e4889e` to `#e58168`. Series 8 is renamed rose to rust (hue 35). The closest pair is now 10.5 in light and 10.2 in dark (was 8.9 and 8.8). No token is added or removed.
- **New: `capsomerMoreTheme`** in `capsomer/enarratio-theme`, the purple family under prefers-contrast: more, for `stylesheet(capsomerTheme, { highContrast: capsomerMoreTheme })`. `css/enarratio.css` maps `--enarratio-font-size-mark` to `max(var(--fs-detail), 0.75rem)`, so mark text follows the reader's text size.
- **Chart frame: a layout around Enarratio's figure.** Changed HTML contract: `.cap-chart` is a `div` holding `.cap-chart-figure` (Enarratio's `figure.enarratio`, with its own title, finding, summary, source, CSV link and "Show data") and anything after it; `data-head="inline"` puts the subtitle at the end of the title's line. No data is `figure.cap-chart[data-state="nodata"]` with `figcaption.cap-chart-title` and `p.cap-chart-reason`. Removed classes and attributes: `.cap-chart-head`, `.cap-chart-meta`, `.cap-chart-summary`, `.cap-chart-body`, `.cap-chart-data`, `.cap-chart-show`, `.cap-chart-hide`, `data-summary="hidden"`, `data-table="own"`, and the `h2` to `h4` title (Enarratio's title is the figure's `figcaption`). New classes: `.cap-chart-figure`, `.cap-chart-reason`.
- **Chart frame, server.** `frameHtml(figure, { inline, after })` (was `frameHtml({ id, title, summary, chart, table, tableLabel, open, meta, level })`) and new `noDataHtml(title, reason)`. Removed: `tableHtml`, `numericColumns`, the `escapeHtml` re-export and the `DataTable` type (Enarratio's `dataTableBlock`, `escapeHtml` and `DataTable` replace them).
- **Chart frame, React: redrawn to its width.** `ChartFrame({ draw, chart, width, minWidth, noData, inline, onDraw, className, children })` draws at 640 on the server and before the first measurement, then redraws at its own width (ResizeObserver, 40 px steps), keeps "Show data" open across redraws and shows Enarratio's refusal in the chart's place. It replaces Foxhound's `EnarratioFigure` and the Portal's `EnarFigure` and `EnarChart`. Removed props: `title`, `summary`, `table`, `tableLabel`, `open`, `meta`, `hideSummary`, `level`. Removed: `ChartData` (use Enarratio's `dataTableBlock`). New export: `drawWidth`, `DEFAULT_WIDTH`.
- **Uptime strip: Enarratio's figure in the frame.** Changed HTML contract: the strip is `uptimeStrip()`'s figure (subtitle "99.4% up over 7 days") in `.cap-chart[data-head="inline"]`, the legend after it; the inline form is `uptimeStrip({ inline: true })`. Removed classes: `.cap-uptime`, `.cap-uptime-title`, `.cap-uptime-figure`, `.cap-uptime-summary`. Renamed: legend key `data-key="partly"` ("Partly down") to `data-key="degraded"` ("Degraded"); the keys are drawn as Enarratio draws its slots (heights, a dashed outline for no data).
- **Stat tile.** The sparkline is `sparkline({ inline: true })`, colored by the tile's tone through `--enarratio-series-1: currentColor`; "Show data" is Enarratio's `dataTableBlock`. Changed HTML contract: the hidden sentence is a `.cap-sr-only` span (was `p.cap-chart-summary`), and the tile frame drops `data-summary="hidden"`.
- **Usage meter: its numbers as a table.** New `usageTable(items)` in `capsomer/behavior/usage-meter`; React `UsageMeter` adds Enarratio's "Show data" under the legend (`tableLabel`, default "Usage against free limits"). Changed HTML contract: `.cap-usage` gains a `.cap-chart` holding the disclosure.
- **The components that use Enarratio import it**: `chart-frame.ts`, `stat-tile.react.tsx` and `usage-meter.react.tsx` need `enarratio` (0.4) installed, as every site that draws charts already has it.
- **Showcase site.** The chart frame, uptime strip, stat tile and usage meter pages render Enarratio live when the site is built (`examples.build.ts`, filling `<!-- build:name -->` in `examples.html`) and load `enarratio/enhance`; nothing is pasted. The chart frame's page is a gallery of every kind and state, the React frame's redraw and refusal, and every family's eight series.

## 0.52.0

The posts list posts the content kit's own intent names and shows what site-api v0.17.0 adds, each only where the kit offers it, each a form post that works with no script.

- **Changed HTML contract: the tag intents are the kit's names.** The bulk bar posts `intent=tag-add` and `intent=tag-remove` (were `add-tag` and `remove-tag`, which site-api's `runPostsIntent` refused). `PostIntent` changes to match. A host that translated the old names drops the translation.
- **Trash** (`offers.trash`): a Trash status tab (`trashed=1` in the address, `PostsQuery.trashed`, `counts.trash`) with "Posts in Trash are deleted for good 30 days after they were moved there." (`trashLine`); new intents `trash` (Move to Trash, in the bar and the row menu, replacing Delete there) and `restore` (in the Trash), each the other's Undo; `delete` is offered in the Trash only, as Delete for good, behind the same typed count. With script, focus goes from a post that leaves to the next post's title, the table or the current tab. New `PostRow.trashedAt`; "Trash is empty" as the empty state. New class `.cap-posts-trash-note`.
- **Filters** (`offers.filters`, the contract's `contentFilters`): Author (`author`, a select of `offers.authors`) and Published from and to (`from`, `to`, date fields) in the GET form, each where the site applies it; Clear filters beside Show once any filter is set (`NO_FILTERS`, `filtered` now counts every one).
- **Authors and excerpt** on a row (`PostRow.authors`, the primary first, and `PostRow.excerpt`): "By Dustin Edwards with Julie Edwards" on the path's line (`authorLine`), the excerpt on a two-line quiet line under it. New classes `.cap-posts-authors`, `.cap-posts-excerpt`.
- **Rename** (`offers.rename`): Rename… in the row menu opens a dialog for the new slug that says the old address will redirect, posting the new intent `rename` with `newId` (and `versions`); with no script it links to the editor. New class `.cap-posts-rename`.
- **Preview links** (`offers.previewLinks`): Get a preview link posts the new intent `preview-link`; `IntentResult.previewLink` (`{ url, expiresAt }`, new) shows in a status region named "Preview link", with Copy and the expiry in the reader's zone. New classes `.cap-posts-preview`, `.cap-posts-preview-row`. `useIntents` returns `latest`, the last result.
- **New view data**, each optional: `PostOffers.trash`, `rename`, `previewLinks`, `filters` (`PostFilter[]`, new type), `authors`; `PostsQuery.author`, `from`, `to`, `trashed`.
- **Media library doc**: the inspector shows a caption where the site keeps one (since site-api v0.10.0); the line that said the contract has none is fixed.

## 0.51.0

The sites' own faces swap in without moving text.

- **Site layers name their fallback faces.** foxing's DM Sans, DM Mono and Fraunces stacks, julieedwards.info's Open Sans and Montserrat, and txasm's Inter now list the matching `… Fallback` face second, so a site that also imports `capsomer/fallback-fonts.css` swaps fonts without moving text. A site that does not import it gets the next face in the stack, as before. Each such layer's header says to import it. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.50.0

Font swaps no longer move text on the sites that load their own faces (the speed and caching pass, 2026-10-31).

- **Fallbacks for the sites' Fontsource faces: `capsomer/fallback-fonts.css` (new export).** One @font-face fallback for each face the sites load themselves, so the swap to the face lands on the same lines: "Montserrat Fallback" and "Open Sans Fallback" (julieedwards.info; bsw's Open Sans), "Inter Fallback" (txasm), "Fraunces Fallback", "Space Grotesk Fallback" and "JetBrains Mono Fallback" (germomics; foxing's Fraunces), and the DM Sans, DM Mono and Source Serif 4 rules README listed to paste. Generated by `tokens/fonts.mjs` from `MEASURED`, which gains the six faces read with `@capsizecss/unpack` from @fontsource 5.3.0's latin 400 files (Fraunces's variable default is wght 900, so it was read at wght 400, opsz 18). `npm run check` refuses a stale file. Checked in Chromium with Liberation Sans and Mono standing in for Arial and Courier New: at 360, 600 and 840 px each fallback set a paragraph on the same lines and height as its face, where plain Arial or Courier New took fewer, except Inter at exactly 16px, whose line box rounds 1px shorter on the same lines (15, 17, 18 and 20px match). Fraunces's Georgia fallback was not checked: no Georgia or Gelasio here. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.49.0

A table between a phone and a laptop no longer scrolls sideways (Dustin, 2026-10-30).

- **New reflow width: `data-reflow="medium"`.** Rows become cards below 860 px of the region, for an app table that fits a laptop but not a 1024 px tablet beside a sidebar. `""` (640 px) and `"wide"` (1100 px) are unchanged. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.48.0

The error page takes a site's own words.

- **Error page.** `ErrorPage` takes `title`, `text` and `homeLabel`, which replace the default words for this error, and `as` (`"main"`, the default, or `"div"` for a Layout that already has the page's `main`). julieedwards.info's 404 words are Carrel copy in D1, so it could not use the page before. No HTML contract changes for existing markup.

## 0.47.0

One error page, theme-color tags a React Layout can use, and the axe helpers written up for sites (the shared-code audit, 2026-10-30).

- **Error page (new component, `capsomer/react/error-page`).** `ErrorPage({ error, home, siteHeader, siteFooter, children, dev })` is a React Router root ErrorBoundary's page: the failed Empty as the page's `h1`, plain words for 404, 410 and everything else, Go to the home page, Try again (a link to the same address) for a server error, `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`, and the stack only with `dev`. The site's header and footer are slots, so the skip link and landmarks stay. `errorTitle(error, siteName)` is the root `meta`'s title on the error path; `errorWords(error)` gives the status, title and sentence. New classes: `.cap-error-page`, `.cap-error-page-stack` (`capsomer/error-page.css`, with `empty.css`). It replaces the eight sites' own ErrorBoundaries.
- **Empty's title can be a heading.** React `Empty` takes `titleAs` (`"p"`, the default, `"h1"`, `"h2"` or `"h3"`). No HTML contract changes for existing markup.
- **theme-color as descriptors.** `themeColorTags(choice, ground)` in `capsomer/theme-server` returns `{ name, content, media }[]`, and React `<ThemeColor choice ground />` in `capsomer/react/theme-switch` renders them in a Layout's head, so dustinedwards.info, julieedwards.info and germomics stop hand-writing the pair. `themeColorMeta` is now those tags as HTML, byte for byte as before.
- **README: Accessibility test for a site.** How a site scans its home, a content page and its 404 in both themes with `capsomer/testing` (`eachTheme`, `expectNoAxeViolations`).
- **Showcase site only.** Its `_headers` is written by site-runtime's `headersFile` (pin v0.32.0); the page policy moves to a second `/*` rule, the same headers on every path. Nothing in the package changes.

## 0.46.0

Hover prefetch for the hydrated React Router apps, through one shared link.

- **New: `capsomer/react/link`.** `Link` and `NavLink` are React Router's own, with the same props, prefetching on hover and focus by default (`prefetch="intent"`: the route's modules and loader data, which is what a client navigation uses, where speculation rules would fetch HTML it never reads). A link that loads a whole document (`reloadDocument`), opens elsewhere (`target` other than `_self`), downloads, leaves the app, points at a file (site-runtime's `FILE_EXTENSIONS`) or goes under site-runtime's `NEVER_PREFETCH` renders with `prefetch="none"`, whatever it asked for, because a prefetch runs the target's loader. `/admin` prefetches only from a page already under `/admin`. `LinkPrefetch` (`<LinkPrefetch exclude={["/demos/:product"]}>`) adds an app's own paths that act on a GET; `:name` and `*` stand for one segment.
- **New: `capsomer/behavior/link`.** `mayPrefetch`, the rule above, and `underPath`, the path match it uses.
- **New optional peers**: `react-router` (^8.0.0) and `@dustinedwards/site-runtime` (>=0.29.0), needed only by these two modules. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.45.0

No dashboard needs a sideways slider (Dustin, 2026-10-30).

- **Table cells wrap.** Body and header cells no longer keep `white-space: nowrap`; text wraps at spaces (`overflow-wrap: break-word`), so a table fits its region instead of scrolling. The scroll region stays as the fallback for a table that cannot fit even wrapped; `data-reflow` still turns rows into cards there. A host that needs a cell on one line sets it itself. Changed HTML contract: none.
- **An admin page never grows wider than the window.** `.cap-admin-page` takes `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)`, so a wide child scrolls or reflows inside its panel; before, a table wider than the page pushed the whole page sideways. No HTML contract changes.
- **No stray scrollbar on a tab strip.** `.cap-tabs-list` sets `overflow-y: hidden` beside its `overflow-x: auto`; the line variant's underline overhung a pixel, and Windows drew a tiny vertical scrollbar on the strip. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.44.2

- **txasm's and Foxing's faces lead with the self-hosted variable files.** Both sites now serve their fonts from Fontsource instead of the Google Fonts stylesheet, and Fontsource names a variable file with "Variable" after the family. The txasm layer's `--ui` starts with "Inter Variable"; Foxing's `--ui` and `--font-ui` start with "DM Sans Variable" and `--display` with "Fraunces Variable". The old names stay next in each stack. No token or class is removed or renamed, and no HTML contract changes.

## 0.44.1

- **A standalone app's letterhead keeps the stripe's height.** The stripe is painted in the header's own color instead of removed, so Foxhound's header, its content and the page under it sit exactly where they do in every family admin. The 0.43.0 `--header-h` override for standalone apps is gone. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.44.0

- **The account button's name tip no longer sits over its open menu, even when moving along the header's icons.** Once one tip is up, the next icon's shows at once, before the panel's own hover opened the account menu, so 0.42.0's check never ran. Opening a header panel now hides its button's tip (`wirePanel`). No HTML contract changes.
- **View site appears once in an admin.** The letterhead's second row shows View site from 768px, where the account button sits in the bar, so the account menu leaves out the row that goes to the same address. On a phone the account is in the menu sheet, which keeps the row. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.43.0

- **Foxhound's admin takes the family letterhead, without the agency.** `FAMILY_APPS` gives Foxhound `names: ["Foxhound", "Admin"]` and the new `standalone: true`: a commercial app outside the Dustin Edwards network, whose letterhead shows "Foxhound Admin" with no "Dustin Edwards" over it and no stripe of the family color on top. The admin shell root carries `data-standalone` for such an app (new attribute).
- **The header mark follows the strip.** The admin shell's logo takes the current app's mark from `apps` before the family default, so an app that passes its own strip mark (Foxhound's hound head) shows it in the header too. No HTML contract changes.

## 0.42.0

- **A tooltip no longer shows over its own open menu.** The shared popover skips a tip whose trigger has `aria-expanded="true"`, so the account button's name tip stays hidden while the account menu is open (the site header and the admin shell). No HTML contract changes.

## 0.41.0

A sign-in code in the magic-link mail, for a link that opens in another browser than the one that asked.

- **`magicLink` takes an optional `code`.** With it, the mail says "Or enter this code on the page where you asked:" under the button, shows the code in the code block (the text part puts it on its own indented line), and the expiry and ignore lines cover the link and the code. Without it the mail is unchanged. The subject never carries the code, so a lock-screen preview does not show it. site-runtime 0.29.0's `./sign-in` sends one with every sign-in link.

## 0.40.0

The media library's ideas from site-api v0.13.0 (v0.13.1 for the rows' orientation), each shown only where the kit offers it, each a form post that works with no script.

- **Duplicates**: an upload or import of bytes the site holds links the file already stored under the result (`filesToOpen`, new, read from the kit's `IntentOutcome.duplicate`, new); the inspector lists the file's twins; the Duplicates lens.
- **Usage**: `MediaRecord.usedCount` (new) gives a list's tiles the Unattached flag at 0 (`flagsFor`); Most used as a sort (`MediaQuery.sort` gains `used`, `SORT_DIR.used`).
- **Filters**: Type and Orientation as link chips under a Filter disclosure (`MediaQuery.kind` and `orientation`, new, in the address; `KIND_WORDS`, `ORIENTATION_WORDS`). The contract has no date filter, so none is offered.
- **Import from a web address** under upload, posting `import` with `url` and `alt`.
- **Credit and license** in the inspector, posting `credit`, with a note when the license has ended; the License ended lens. New `MediaRecord` fields: `credit`, `license`, `sourceUrl`, `licenseExpires`, `takenAt`, `twins`.
- **Turns**: Rotate left and right, Flip horizontally and vertically, posting `transform` with `op`; on a site whose addresses carry the size, the inspector says a quarter turn is saved as a new file, and the result links the copy (`IntentOutcome.copyId`, new).
- **History**: the file's earlier bytes with Go back to this, posting `revert` with `to` (`MediaData.history`, `MediaRevision`, new).
- **Live updates**: with script and the new `changes` prop (the kit's `loadMediaChanges`), the library polls from `page.changeCursor` every 15 seconds while visible, puts changed files in place, takes deleted ones out, and says so (`applyChanges`, `KitMediaChanges`, new).
- **Shrink**: Shrink to the site's size in the inspector, and Shrink all on this page on the Oversized lens, posting `resize` (the kit lists every file and asks first); Keep it at full size (`resize=no`) on upload and import where the site shrinks uploads.
- **Embedded metadata**: the inspector shows when a picture was taken and offers its caption as an editable starting point for alt text.
- **Suggestions**: Suggest tags and alt links to `suggest=1` (`MediaQuery.suggest`, new); each suggested tag and the suggested alt text is accepted on its own, never applied unasked (`MediaData.suggestions`, `MediaSuggestions`, new).
- `MediaOffers` gains `duplicates`, `usage`, `filters`, `import`, `credit`, `transform`, `history`, `live`, `resize`, `resizePass`, `embedded` and `suggestions`, all optional; `mediaDataOfKit` and `recordOfKit` map the new fields; `inspectorOffers` names the new ones. `MediaRecord.orientation` (new) is the kit's (site-api v0.13.1 rows), never worked out here. New helpers: `turnMakesCopy`, `oversized`, `licenseEnded`, `dayWords`, `momentWords`.
- `MediaInspector` takes `more` (new): sections after the picture tools, built from the inspector's own form or nested group; a nested tool now also sends text areas, selects and checked boxes.
- New classes: `.cap-media-lib-opened`, `.cap-media-lib-accept`, `.cap-media-lib-history` (with `-what`, `-facts`), `.cap-media-lib-revert`. The ended-license note is the banner stylesheet's `.cap-alert`, already needed by the picture tools.

## 0.39.0

One mapping from the content kit to the media library, so Carrel and every site admin read the kit's page the same way.

- **`mediaDataOfKit(kit, { usedHref, view })`** (new, `capsomer/behavior/media-library`) turns site-api's `loadMedia` answer into `MediaData`: each row a `MediaRecord` (id as key, the file name or id as name, the site's own address as `url` and the kit's absolute `src` as `thumb`, image or document by type, Trash as `binned`, version, tags, caption and focal point), tiles from a list marked `usedUnknown`, the inspected file with where it is used linking through `usedHref`, and the tag chips from the page's rows with their counts on this page. `view` merges the drawing from the address (`readMediaView`) and the picker's `pick`. `recordOfKit(row, used?)` (new) maps one file. `KitMediaRow` and `KitMediaData` (new) are the kit's shapes, typed here so Capsomer imports nothing from site-api.
- **Empty Trash**: in Trash, with the delete role and a site with a trash, a button posts the kit's `empty-trash`, which names every file and asks for the count before anything goes (site-api v0.12.0). Carrel's own library had it.
- **A post is never sent twice.** With `submit` and a router's `Form` (React Router's prevents the default and posts itself), the content components' script path also posted the same form, so a write ran twice and the second hit a version conflict. The script path now leaves a form whose submit was already handled. To get the in-place path, pass a `Form` that is the router's for `method="get"` and a plain form for posts.
- **A component's own list keeps its layout in a dialog.** The dialog's rules for prose lists and sheet links (`.cap-dialog-body ul`, `.cap-dialog-body nav ul`, `.cap-dialog-body nav a`) are now under `:where`, so chips and the media grid in a dialog (the image picker) are no longer stacked as full-width rows with bullets. Unclassed lists and links in a dialog look as before.
- **An editor can move files to Trash and restore them**, as the content kit allows (site-api `Permissions.edit`: trash and restore are reversible). The bulk bar and the inspector offered them only with the delete role, so Carrel's Editors lost them; Delete for good and Replace still need it.

## 0.38.0

Media library parity with dustinedwards.info's own library, so Carrel can be the one place a site's media is managed.

- **The site's lenses as chips.** `MediaOffers.lenses` (new, optional: the kit's `offers.lenses`, site-api v0.6.0 `capabilities.mediaLenses`) gives a chip for each of the contract's lenses the site names, in words: Unattached, No alt text, Over 1 MB, with what the open one means under the chips. A host's own lenses (`extra.lenses`) follow; a count in `extra.lenses` for a contract lens shows on its chip. Lenses are shown in the library only, not in Trash. New: `lensChips`, `LENS_WORDS`.
- **Sort, direction, grouping and tile size, as links** under a Display disclosure beside Grid and List: Newest, A to Z, Largest (the contract's `added`, `name`, `size`), Descending or Ascending, grouping by None, Folder or Month, and tile size S, M or L. `MediaQuery` gains `sort`, `dir`, `group` and `size` (new HTML contract: those query parameters). A sort starts in its own direction and its default is left out of the address (`SORT_DIR`, `dirOf`); a layout, size or grouping change keeps the page and the open file (`withMedia`). Where the kit sorted only the page (`page.sortedOnPage`) the library says so.
- **Groups by folder or month**, drawn from the page's rows: the folder before the last "/" of each id, or the upload month in UTC, through the media grid's `groups` (`groupRows`, `folderOf`, `monthOf`). Page-local, as dustinedwards.info's are.
- **Grid or List and the tile size are remembered in this browser** (`cap-media-layout`, `cap-media-size`); a link that names one still wins. `readMediaView(searchParams)` reads `layout`, `size` and `group` for a host to merge with the kit's `readMediaQuery`.
- **Keyboard: quick search and Esc.** Ctrl K, Cmd K or "/" opens the command menu over the host's matches when it passes `search` (new prop), with Enter opening a file; without it the keys put focus in the search field. `shortcuts={false}` (new prop) leaves the keys to the host. Esc on a tile clears the selection, then closes the open file's details. The keys are listed under a Keyboard shortcuts disclosure.
- New classes: `.cap-media-lib-display`, `.cap-media-lib-tool-label`, `.cap-media-lib-note`, `.cap-media-lib-keys`. The library now also needs the disclosure, listbox, command-menu and shortcuts stylesheets.
- **Focal point, crop, replace and caption in the inspector**, ported from Carrel's own media code and run as host intents. `MediaInspector` takes `tools` and `onTool` (new); `MediaLibrary` builds them from the kit's offers (`MediaOffers.focus`, `caption`, `replace`, `replaceKeepsSize`, new and optional) and takes `bytesHref` (new) for Crop. The focal point posts `intent=focus` with `x` and `y` in percent or `clear=yes`; replace is a multipart form posting `intent=replace` with `file`; Crop, loaded with `React.lazy` only when pressed, saves the cut as a new file (`upload`, then opens it) or in place (`replace`). Where the site refuses a replacement of another size (site-api `mediaReplaceKeepsSize`, through `offers.replaceKeepsSize`), replace names the size it must be and a crop is never saved in place. `MediaRecord.focus` (new). New helpers: `fitRect`, `centered`, `ratioOf`, `cropName`, `focusPercents`, `CROP_SHAPES`, `CROPPABLE`, `bareType`. New classes: `.cap-media-tool`, `.cap-media-tool-actions`, `.cap-media-summary-note`, `.cap-media-focus*`, `.cap-media-crop*`. The tools need the dialog, select and banner stylesheets as well.
- **Changed HTML contract: the inspector's forms post the content kit's names.** `version` is now `versions`, in every inspector form and the library's autosave; the form-mode intents `save-alt` and `save-tags` are now `alt` and `tags`, and a site with a caption but no title posts `caption` (with a title, still `save-details`). A host that read the old names must read the new ones.
- **Changed HTML contract: the bulk bar and the autosaving inspector post the content kit's own intents** (site-api `runMediaIntent`), so a host needs no translation layer. The bulk bar's tag actions are `tag-add` and `tag-remove` (were `add-tags` and `remove-tags`) and its field is `tags` (was `tag`); `MediaIntent` changes to match. Each media tile's box carries a hidden `version:<id>` field in the bulk form, the version the page saw, which the kit reads for the ticked ids (site-api: a version per box). The autosaving inspector no longer posts `save`: it posts `alt`, `caption` and `tags` for the fields that changed, one after another, each on the version the last write made (`IntentOutcome.version`, new, site-api's `Outcome.version`). `ContentBulkBar` takes `tagName` (new, "tag" by default) for its Tag field's name; posts and mentions are unchanged.
- **Fixes from the screenshots.** File sizes on tiles and list rows read "1.8 MB", not "1 . 8 MB" (tabular figures gave the point a digit's width; `.cap-media-meta`, `.cap-media-count` and `.cap-media-lib-count` use proportional figures). An open Display panel no longer pulls the count and Grid or List down to the middle of the row (the bar aligns on the first line's baseline). Groups by folder or month are spaced apart as sections (`--space-7` between them).
- **Key caps draw the arrows as ←, →, ↑ and ↓ and Escape as Esc** (`keyCaps`, so the shortcut sheet, the command menu and the media library's Keyboard shortcuts all do). An arrow's cap names it for a screen reader and in a tooltip ("Left arrow"); `capName(cap)` (new) gives the name.
- **Quick search copies an address**: each match has a Copy the address option beside the one Enter opens, and the library says it copied.
- `useIntents` also returns `run(form)`, which runs an intent and resolves to its result (a failed result when it throws), for a host action that must wait for the outcome.

## 0.37.0

Fallback fonts for any site's faces, and scheduled updates in the posts list (site-api v0.10.0).

- **Size-matched fallbacks for a site's own faces.** New entry `capsomer/fonts` (`tokens/fallback-fonts.mjs`, with types): `fallbackFontFace({ family, metrics, local })` writes the @font-face rule tokens/fonts.mjs writes for the family's faces, `fallbackName(family)` its name, `LOCAL` the local faces' metrics and their metric twins, and `MEASURED` the faces already measured. New local faces for serif: Times New Roman (with Liberation Serif and Tinos) and Georgia (with Gelasio). Pre-measured: DM Sans, DM Mono and Source Serif 4, with the rules ready to paste in README.md (DM Sans matches foxhound's hand-made rule to the hundredth).
- **`capsomer fallback-font <file.woff2> [--family] [--local]`** measures a site's font file with `@capsizecss/unpack` (a new optional peer dependency) and prints the rule. `css/fonts.css` is unchanged. `tokens/fallback-fonts.mjs` credits Astro's method in one line: it copies no Astro code, so it carries no MIT notice.
- **A live post's scheduled update shows in its status, on one line**: `PostRow.scheduledUpdate` (`{ publishAt, href }`) reads "Published · update in 2 days", as a scheduled post now reads "Scheduled · in 3 days" (`rowStatus`, new). The row's menu links to the update. With `offers.scheduledUpdates` (new, optional) and the publish role it adds Reschedule… (a small dialog: the new time in the viewer's zone, posted in UTC as `reschedule` with `publishAt`) and Cancel the update (a danger item that asks first, then posts `cancel-schedule`). With no script both link to the update's page. New: `ScheduledUpdateSummary`, `scheduledTime`, `rowStatus`, `localInputValue`, `isoFromLocal`; `PostIntent` gains the two intents.
- **`PostScheduledUpdate`**, in `capsomer/react/posts-list`: the page of one waiting update, from site-api's `loadScheduledUpdate` (new type `ScheduledUpdateView` in `capsomer/content`). A page head with the title, when it goes live in the viewer's zone and its actions (Cancel the update set apart), then "Live post · on the site since …" and "Scheduled update · goes live …" over the update beside the live post in the draft compare and the patch in a disclosure.
- **The posts list's columns hug their content**: the title takes the spare width, so the status, the date and the menu sit together at the end. Changed: `.cap-posts-title`, `.cap-posts-status`, `.cap-posts-updated` widths.
- **On a phone the posts list is cards**, one per post, through the table's reflow (`data-reflow` on `.cap-posts-wrap`, new), instead of a table that scrolls sideways. Host columns' cells carry `data-label` and `.cap-posts-extra-cell` (new).
- **An open row menu is no longer clipped by the table's region**: the region lets it out while it is open, in place of 12rem of padding under the table.
- **Draft compare, on one line.** The view switch, the counts, the key ("Added", "Removed") and the jump buttons share one row; the views' explanations are the switch's description for a screen reader (Changed: `.cap-compare-views-help` is gone, the key's words beside each mark are gone, the jump list moves into `.cap-compare-bar`, "Previous" and "Next" say "change" to a screen reader only). Each side's name is one line over its column instead of a card, and `heads: false` (new option) leaves the names to the page. The text is in the reading context's face (`--face`), not always `--prose`: the UI face in an app, the serif inside `data-context="prose"`.
- **Draft compare: no space before punctuation after an insertion or a deletion.** "nothing." becoming "nothing but notes." showed the before side as "nothing ." (`diffWords` kept the change's leading space on both sides).
- **An exact time and the compare's counts use proportional figures.** Tabular figures spaced out the comma and the colon in a sentence ("10 : 00").
- **`useIntents`** returns `ask(request)`, the confirm dialog for a one-way intent the kit runs at once; `ConfirmSpec.cancelLabel` (new, optional) names the button that backs out. **`PageHead`** takes `headingLevel`. **`FormMenuItem.onSelect`**: a link item that script turns into an action (with no script the link goes to the page that does it); only such a link may be a danger item.
- **An exact time is written in American English** (found building Carrel on Capsomer): "November 3, 2026, 8:00 AM CST (2:00 PM UTC)", where it was "3 November 2026, 08:00 GMT-6 (14:00 UTC)". The zone is its American short name ("CST", "CDT", "EST", "PDT"); a zone with none keeps its British one where there is one ("BST", "CET"), else the offset ("GMT+9"). A date in a row is month first too ("September 12", "December 2, 2025"). The clock is the American 12-hour one in both parts, with proportional figures as before. New export: `zoneName` (time). Changed: the text of `exact`, `exactParts`, `relative` and `day`, so every component that shows a time (posts list, publish gate, draft compare, history, flags).
- **React `Time` no longer breaks hydration** (found through Carrel's publish gate). With `format="exact"` the browser drew the UTC part's span where the server had sent one text, so React threw #418. Until hydration is done the time is now drawn in UTC on both sides, then redraws in the viewer's zone. Every component that renders `Time` (publish gate, posts list, history, flags, mentions, the admin shell) gets the fix; an app's own exact-time stand-in can go. Also: the meter's default "1,000 of 5,000" is written in en-US whatever the server's or browser's locale.
- **American dates across the kit and the showcase.** People and roles writes "Ends May 14, 2027", "Ends Dec 18, 2026, in 18 days" and "Oct 2, 2026, 2:52 PM UTC" (`dayText`, `timeText`; short months, so the table still fits its column without scrolling); the history list's stamps read "Oct 2, 2:40 PM UTC" and a run "2:02 PM to 2:40 PM UTC" (`stamp`, `dayOf`, `rangeText`). Every component's sample HTML now writes dates month first ("Tue, Sep 29", "September 28, 2026, 6:05 PM").
- **Publish gate on a phone: the time a site went live no longer sits on the site's name.** The row list's narrow rule for a row with a status outranked the gate's own, so the date took rows 2 and 3 beside the title across the full width; it now falls under the detail as the gate meant, an empty one takes no row, and the name and the cause take the row's whole width.
- **Drop zone: `data-accepted-list="off"`** (new; React `acceptedList={false}`) leaves out the zone's own "3 files are ready to upload." and its list, for an app that shows its own upload queue. Refusals are still named and `cap-drop-accepted` still fires; the status region may be left out of the markup. New HTML contract: the attribute; nothing else changes.
- **Banner and alert: actions that wrap start under the words**, aligned with them, instead of at the far end of the box. The glyph hangs in the box's start padding, so the words start at the content's edge, and the actions after the words drop their auto margin (the words grow to fill the line, so on one line the actions still sit at the end).
- **Shared pins:** devkit v0.16.1 (package and workflows), site-runtime v0.26.0.

## 0.36.0

Size-matched fallback fonts, from Astro's Fonts API (MIT, credited in `tokens/fonts.mjs`).

- **The swap to the family faces barely moves the text.** `css/fonts.css` gains "Schibsted Grotesk Fallback" and "Martian Mono Fallback": local Arial and Courier New (and their metric twins Liberation and Arimo or Cousine, for Linux and ChromeOS) scaled to each face's average width, with its ascent, descent and line gap. Checked in Chromium: a 600px paragraph in the fallback breaks on the same 7 lines as Schibsted Grotesk, where plain Arial took 6; Martian Mono's fallback matched its 11 lines exactly. Changed: `--ui`, `--mono`, `--display`, Capsid's `--prose` and the Enarratio theme's fonts list the fallback second.
- **`css/fonts.css` is generated** by `tokens/fonts.mjs` (`npm run tokens`), and `npm run check` refuses a stale file or a font file whose bytes no longer match the metrics measured from it.
- **One font stack.** The UI and mono stacks are written once, as `UI_STACK` and `MONO_STACK` in `tokens/palette-core.mjs`, instead of in four modules and the theme builder. New exports: `UI_STACK`, `MONO_STACK`.

## 0.35.0

Dark mode, designed rather than inverted (Dustin, 2026-10-29: "proceed" on the dark mode plan). Every family's dark theme was already its own OKLCH scale, but it was tuned to WCAG 2's ratio alone, which overrates gray on near-black: dark muted text, status words, button labels, focus rings and control borders passed at about half the APCA contrast of the light theme.

- **The palette is checked against APCA as well as WCAG 2.2.** Every pair now needs its WCAG ratio and an APCA floor: Lc 90 for body text, 75 for accent text, 60 for other text, 45 for the focus ring, 30 for a control's border (`LEVELS` in `tokens/palette-core.mjs`). `npm run check` checks all ten families in both themes and at both contrast levels; the theme builder shows both numbers. New exports: `apca`, `LEVELS`, `strengthen`, `fitNeutrals`, `moreLines`; `familyTheme`, `pairs` and `failures` take a contrast level, and a pair carries `lc` and `lcMin`.
- **Dark text, buttons, rings and borders are stronger.** In dark, muted and dim text reach Lc 60 (purple's muted #96949e is now #b7b5bf), status words Lc 60, accent text Lc 75 (purple #c6b1f2 is #d8c7ff), and the primary fill is lighter so its dark label reaches Lc 60 (purple #9b6ee3 is #c09eff). The focus ring in dark is accent text, not the brand color, which was 3.96:1 and Lc 30 for teal. Control borders reach Lc 30. The light themes are unchanged apart from amber.
- **Site layer: the Capsid explorer keeps the teal family's darker ground in dark** (Dustin, 2026-10-29, asked whether the flat dark page was meant: "You tell me"). Its dark ground was the surface color (#111919), so panels on the surface did not show against the page; dark mode shows depth with lighter surfaces, so its dark ground is now the family's #070f0f. Light keeps the surface as the ground.
- **Amber's light button** fills lighter (#daa73f) so its dark label reaches Lc 60 (it was 43.5), and keeps the 3:1 step 9 (#b68508) as its edge. New token: `--primary-line`, the primary button's edge; it equals `--primary` everywhere else. Amber's light accent text moves from #8a5c06 to #845802 to reach Lc 75. Changed: the primary button's edge reads `--primary-line`.
- **Increase contrast.** Under `prefers-contrast: more` every family, the pinned themes (`themes.css`) and each site's own neutrals take a generated stronger set: muted text, status words and links at Lc 75, rings at Lc 60, borders at Lc 45 and visible dividers. Only the tokens that change are written. The Light and Dark switch is unchanged.
- **The manual's signs stay dark in dark.** The running head's sign variant, the signs, the direction strip and the checklist and cue strips were `--text` panels with `--ground` type, so in dark they turned into near-white slabs. They now take `--sign`, `--on-sign` and `--sign-edge`: the same dark panel in light, and in dark a raised panel of light type with an edge. New tokens: `--sign`, `--on-sign`, `--sign-edge`.
- **`--dim` is text only.** The bulk bar, the message region and the theme switch drew their edge in `--dim`; they now use `--line-strong`, which is checked at 3:1 on the ground as well. Changed: those three components' borders.
- **Flat images.** `.cap-img-flat` gives a screenshot or a diagram on white an edge and, in dark, dims it a little (`--img-filter`, new) so it does not glow.
- **The browser bar.** `themeColorMeta(choice, { light, dark })` in `capsomer/theme-server` writes `<meta name="theme-color">` tags, and the theme switch keeps them on the chosen theme's ground.
- **Site layer.** Foxhound's dark text, muted, dim and control border, and Foxing's dark muted and dim, are lifted to the APCA floors in their own hues (Foxhound's dark border was #4a4c44, 2.02:1; its waiver is gone). Changed: those values in `tokens/sites.mjs`.

## 0.34.1

- **A short page's letterhead sits on the same pixels as a long page's.** A page with a letterhead keeps the scrollbar's room (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`), so a page too short to scroll no longer shifts the band half a scrollbar (7px in Chrome on Windows) to the right. Dustin saw it on the dustinedwards.info admin's Drafts list (2026-10-28).

## 0.34.0

- **Home and an admin's breadcrumbs start at one place on every site** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "The home link should be in the same location on all sites and same for the breadcrumbs"). From 1080px a letterhead's first column is at least `--header-names` (19.75rem) wide, so a public page's first link and an admin's first crumb start at x 496 at 1536px whatever the site's name. Before, the links followed the name: Home sat at x 334 on Capsid, x 344 on Capsomer and x 395 on dustinedwards.info. The links' 32px start padding is gone; the column replaces it.
- **An admin's band is one row from 1080px, like a public page's.** The trail ("Components / Shell") sits where a public page has its links, and View site sits at its end, 36px before search. Before, both had a 48px second row. Changed: `--header-h` and `--admin-top` are 88px in an admin from 1080px (136px from 768px to 1080px, where the trail keeps its row).

## 0.33.2

- **An admin's bar reaches the top of the window in the band's color** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "fill the gap above the stripe with the black color used below it"). The 16px above an admin's stripe was the page's ground, which read as a gap over a full-width bar.
- **Site layer: the Portal takes the Capsid site's teal** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "match the theme of the site"), where it was purple. Changed: `capsid-portal`'s family in `tokens/sites.mjs`; the Portal sets `data-family="teal"` on its `<html>`.
- **Site only:** the Scaffold takes the showcase's magenta, so its stripe and accents match the public pages.

## 0.33.1

- **An app's own search keeps the search slot in the letterhead.** An admin that opens a command menu (`onSearch`, an extra with id `search`) had its search icon after the theme, in the inbox's group (the Portal's search sat at x 1240 and its theme at x 1192 at 1536px). It now sits before the theme, where a site's search form sits.
- **An admin's names on a phone.** Under 768px the account avatar moves out of the bar into the menu's sheet, which already lists the account's rows and Sign out, and the names wrap only before the app's name: "Dustin Edwards" over "Admin" on dustinedwards.info, where it read "Dustin", "Edwards", "Admin" on three lines beside four icons.

## 0.33.0

- **An admin's letterhead keeps the public page's pixels** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "it is full screen yet all the components need to stay in place"). The admin band still runs the full width, but from 768px its logo, names, search, theme and second row sit exactly where a public page's do: 16px down from the top, inside the 1280px page column. The inbox and the account take the two slots a public page leaves empty. Before, the logo lined up with the menu (x 64 at 1536px) and the band started at the top of the window. `--header-h` is 136px in an admin from 768px.
- **An admin's names stack as on the public pages**: "Dustin Edwards" over the site's name in bold, then the app's name in the regular weight ("Capsid Portal", "Carrel Desk", "Capsomer Scaffold"). The dustinedwards.info admin is "Dustin Edwards Admin" under the headquarters' empty agency line, so it sits where "Dustin Edwards" does on the public site. Before, the admin showed its name on one line with no agency. New: `FamilyApp.names`.
- **Site only:** the Scaffold (`/admin/`) uses the letterhead, with search and View site.

## 0.32.0

- **One body size for the family page: `--fs-page` (17px)** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "pixel perfect as I move browser tab between websites"). Each site set its own body size, from 14px to 17px; `.cap-page` now sets `--fs-page` and a 1.55 leading, so text inside the page column is the same size on every site. An app's rows keep `--fs-body`. New token: `--fs-page`.
- **The system map's caption keeps its own row.** Its `max-width` let the next item wrap up beside it, so the Capsid explorer set its own rule to undo it; the measure is now padding inside a full-width row.

## 0.31.0

- **Search is an icon, and it answers as you type** (Dustin, 2026-10-28). The letterhead's search box was a box on half the sites (the header differed from tab to tab), opened a second box on dustinedwards.info, and did nothing until Enter elsewhere. Now it is a magnifier icon beside the theme, only on a site with search. The icon links to the search page, so it works with no script; with script it, or "/", opens a field under it, focused. Pass `suggest(query, signal)` (the site's own search; `search.suggest` in React, `enhance(root, { suggest })` for `letterheadHtml` pages) and up to six matches drop down as the person types, then "All results for …". Up and Down mark one, Enter opens it (or, with none marked, the full results page), Esc closes the list and then the field. The field is a WAI-ARIA combobox with the marked match as its `aria-activedescendant`. New: `wireSearch`, `searchHtml`, `SearchHit`, `Suggest`, `SUGGEST_DELAY_MS`. Changed HTML contract: `.cap-site-header-search` now wraps the icon link (`.cap-site-header-search-button`, `data-cap-part="search-trigger"`) and its panel (`.cap-site-header-search-panel`) holding the form; the form is no longer in the band. A site that bound "/" to its own search should drop that binding.
- **The section links sit in the top row where they fit** (Dustin, 2026-10-28). From 1080px a public page's letterhead is one 64px row, the links after the names (`--header-h` drops by 48px there); narrower, they keep their second row. An admin keeps its second row.
- **The family page is 1280px wide** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "it can be wider than it is now"). `--page-max` goes from 1064px to 1280px (a 1248px column). The 1064px came from fitting the reading line in two-thirds of the column, a rule for article pages; most family pages are grids, maps and tables, and it left wide empty margins on a laptop. Running text keeps `--measure`.
- **Running text is up to 80 characters wide** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "66 character line is also stupid, especially since you just widened everything"). `--measure` goes from 66ch to 80ch, the ceiling WCAG 1.4.8 sets for a line of Latin text, about two-thirds of the new column at the 17px body.
- **One set of font files for the family: `capsomer/fonts.css`** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "the footer dustin edwards is not exact in all of the sites"). The footer's name is 700 weight, but Prelum, Carrel and Enarratio each loaded only Schibsted Grotesk 400 and 600 and Capsomer's site 400, 600 and 800, so the browser drew the bold from a different face on each site. `css/fonts.css` declares Schibsted Grotesk (400 to 900), Martian Mono (100 to 800) from one variable file each (Source Serif 4 already came from one variable file everywhere), shipped in `fonts/` (OFL 1.1). Every site loads it instead of choosing its own weights; a site that inlines the CSS copies `fonts/` beside it. New exports: `./fonts.css`, `./fonts/*`.
- **Site layer: dustinedwards.info** no longer names its own faces ("Schibsted Grotesk Web" and the rest) or its own 42rem reading line: it takes the family's faces from `capsomer/fonts.css` and the family `--measure`.
- **The headquarters' name matches the sites' names** (Dustin, 2026-10-28). With no site name, "Dustin Edwards" now takes the site name's size (20px), weight (700) and place, under an empty agency line, where it was the agency's 12.5px. The mark fills one 32px square on every site, so the names start at the same place whatever the mark's shape.
- **Site only:** the header's search answers from the site's search index.

## 0.30.0

- **The letterhead sets `--header-h`** to what it covers when pinned (its stripe and two rows, 120px, and from 768px on a public page the 16px above it), and its scroll padding reads it. A site's sticky parts and anchors read the token instead of working the height out again (dustinedwards.info did).
- **Site layer: the Capsid explorer and the Portal are two sites.** `data-cap-site="capsid"` is now the explorer (capsid.dustinedwards.info, teal): running text in the UI face, and its surface as the ground. The Portal is `data-cap-site="capsid-portal"` with `capsomer/site-capsid-portal.css` (its 13px secondary text on a phone, as before). Changed HTML contract: the Portal's root attribute.
- **The family footer is the same to the pixel on every site** (Dustin, 2026-10-28): it sets its own type (`--fs-body`, 1.5 leading, the UI face) instead of taking the page's body size, which ran from 14px to 17px across the sites.
- **The family arrow (`.cap-go`) moves to `capsomer/utilities.css`**, which `base.css` still imports, so a page that keeps its own base (dustinedwards.info) draws the footer's arrows too. It is now in the `cap.utilities` layer.
- **Site layer: dustinedwards.info** takes its faces (the self-hosted Schibsted Grotesk, Source Serif 4 and Martian Mono), its 42rem reading line, its paper ground and its layers above its own pinned bar (`--z-sticky` 100, `--z-overlay` 800) from Capsomer, where the site had set them itself.

## 0.29.0

- **One page width for the family** (Dustin, 2026-10-28: "why are they all different widths"). The new `--page-max` token (1064px, gutters included) and `--page-gutter` (16px) set it, and the new `.cap-page` class wraps a page's content and footer in it, so every site's letterhead, content and footer sit on the same 1032px column at a wide window. The width comes from the reading line, not taste: the column is the measure (66ch, 686px at the 17px body) divided by two-thirds, GOV.UK's rule that a page's main text takes two-thirds of the column, rounded to the 8px grid. The letterhead takes its width from `--page-max`. Removed: `--header-max` (each site set its own) and `--header-gutter`.
- **Site only:** the front page's column is `--page-max` (it was 76rem).

## 0.28.1

- **The letterhead keeps a 16px gap above it on public pages** (Dustin, 2026-10-27): from 768px the band sits 16px below the top of the window, at rest and pinned, with the page's ground covering the gap and the margins beside it, so it reads as a letterhead on the sheet. `scroll-padding-top` grows to match. The admin's band stays at the top edge.
- **A fade under the pinned band** on public pages, as wide as the window, once the page has scrolled (a scroll timeline; none where unsupported).
- **`letterheadHtml()`** (`capsomer/behavior/site-header`): a public page's letterhead as an HTML string, for static builds and servers (the Prelum specimen, the Enarratio gallery, Capsid's explorer, Carrel's public page), the way `familyFooterHtml()` writes the footer. Capsomer's own site uses it. A mark drawn without its own fill takes the site's color.
- **The family arrow glides** under the pointer or focus: its 200ms slide never ran, because its rule put pseudo-elements inside `:is()`, which matches none, so the arrow jumped.
- **The letterhead stays dark in dark mode** (Dustin, 2026-10-27): the band is the raised dark surface (`--raised`) with light text, instead of flipping to a light band. Its stripe keeps the site's color.
- **Fix: an admin's letterhead row is never empty.** Without a `page` title, its second row now names the current menu entry ("Home", or "Sites / Foxhound"), where before it stood blank, which on a phone left an empty dark band.
- **Fix: a long name wraps on a phone.** "Capsid Portal" beside an admin's four header tools ran under them; under 768px the name now wraps to two lines.
- **Fix: the header's arrows in a page without `base.css`.** The arrow's shape is now the `--cap-arrow` token in `tokens/scale.css` (moved from `css/base.css`), so the Portal, which loads the tokens but not the base, draws View site's arrow instead of a square.
- **Fix: a leader index fits a phone.** A long name or fact wraps inside its row instead of pushing the page 10px wider (Capsid's front page at 360px).
- **Fix: the footer's first social icon names itself inside the screen.** Its name opened centered on the icon and ran off a phone's left edge; the first and last now open inward.

## 0.28.0

- **The letterhead header** (`<SiteHeader variant="letterhead">`, `data-variant="letterhead"`; Dustin, 2026-10-27). One header for the six family sites and their admins: a dark band (light in dark mode) under an 8px stripe of the site's color, a 64px top row with the logo at the left and the names beside it ("Dustin Edwards" small over the site's name), search, the theme and, in an admin, the inbox and account in the corner (their slots stay empty on the public pages, so search and the theme never move); a 48px second row with the section links, Home first, at every width. The band stays at the top of the window while the page scrolls, on public pages and in the admin. On a public page from 768px it sits inside the page's margins, at the content's width (`--header-max`, default 74rem), with the page's ground covering the margins beside it; on a phone it runs edge to edge. In an admin it runs the full width, the logo and where you are lined up with the menu's buttons. Under 480px tall (a phone held sideways, 400% zoom) it scrolls away instead. New props: `variant`, `search`, `trail`, and `agency` and `app` on the logo. New classes: `cap-site-header-names`, `-agency`, `-name`, `-app`, `-search`, `-trail`, `-view`. The default header is unchanged.
- **`AdminShell` takes `letterhead` and `search`**: the header's second row shows where you are and View site, and the page bar drops its breadcrumb. `--admin-top` grows to match.
- **The family arrow** (`--cap-arrow` in `css/base.css`; Dustin, 2026-10-27: "add the arrow"). One drawn arrow, after the arrows on the NYC Transit (1970) and British Rail (1965) signs, used everywhere a link goes somewhere: the signs, View site in an admin's header, and `.cap-go` for a plain link (`data-go="out"` turns it to point up and out, as for a link that leaves the site; `data-go="back"` turns it to point back). Under the pointer or focus it steps 3px the way it points (100ms); with reduced motion it stays still. In forced colors it is drawn in `LinkText`. New: the `--cap-arrow` property and the `.cap-go` class. The family footer's profile links take `.cap-go data-go="out"`.
- **Signs drop their underline** (`.cap-sign`; Dustin, 2026-10-27: no underline on button-like links whose arrow moves). The arrow's step is the hover; with reduced motion the panel lightens instead. Their arrows are now the family arrow, not text glyphs. Changed HTML contract: none.
- **Letterhead header links tint under the pointer**, as Carbon's shell does, and the current page keeps its 2px underline, now inset to the tint. The tint is the band's own `--header-hover`; it fades in where motion is allowed.
- **Site only: search** at `/search/` (Dustin, 2026-10-27: "we have a lot of components"). The header's search box goes there; it finds components, the headings in their docs, the front page's parts and the theme builder, and links to the heading that matched. The build writes the index (`/search.json`) with Prelum's `staticSearchIndex`, and the page ranks it in the browser with `staticSearch`. The page is noindex; the front page's JSON-LD names it as the site's SearchAction. Prelum v0.15.0.
- **Site only: the site wears the letterhead.** Its links are Home, Color, Type, Components, Defaults, Install and Make a theme; the running head is back to the plain one, and the front page's reads "1 Overview" so it does not repeat the Home link.

## 0.27.0

- **Magenta moves from hue 330 to 334** (seed #b24fac to #b64da6; Dustin, 2026-10-26, after a search of every hue for the six public sites). Its closest neighbors were purple (CIEDE2000 11.9) and rose (14.6); now both are about 13.3. No token is renamed; magenta's values change a little.
- **Carrel and Enarratio join the family** (Dustin, 2026-10-26). Two new families at purple's lightness and strength: olive (seed #768602, hue 118) for Carrel's public site and rose (seed #c94574, hue 2) for Enarratio's gallery (`data-family="olive"`, `data-family="rose"`; `capsomer/family-olive.css`, `capsomer/family-rose.css`, both in `capsomer/families.css`, `--family-olive` and `--family-rose` in `family-marks.css`). Every pair passes in both themes. `EmailFamily` gains both names; the theme builder has both as presets.
- **The family footer's strip lists Carrel and Enarratio** (`FAMILY_SITES`: `carrel`, Writing, olive; `enarratio`, Charts, rose). The strip is two columns on a phone, three from 40rem and six from 72rem, so six sites never wrap as five and one.
- **Carrel's signed-in app is Desk** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: named the way Capsid's is the Portal). The footer's Workspace link reads Desk and goes to https://desk.dustinedwards.info (accessible name "Desk, Carrel's writing app (requires sign-in)", hover "Carrel Desk"); the admin strip's tile reads Carrel Desk and goes there too. Its `id` stays `carrel`. Changed HTML contract: the link's text, name and address.
- **Site only: the public site in the NASA manual style, in magenta** (Dustin, 2026-10-26). The page root is `data-family="magenta"`; each page opens with a running head, the section titles are numbered parts, and the footer is a letterhead, all from `manual.css`. The Supergraphics tiles and the Lohse and Albers pieces stay. The family preview now always sets `data-family`, so purple shows purple on a magenta page.
- **Site only: component pages laid out like shadcn/ui's docs** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: "our component pages should be like theirs"). Every component sits in a list on the left with a filter, and the page's sections on the right, with the one you are reading marked. In the middle: the name with Copy page (its doc as Markdown) and previous and next, the first example live with its markup under it, Installation (HTML and CSS, React), every other example live with its markup, then the doc. Each example is the states page showing only that example (`?example=<heading id>`), framed at its own height and in the page's theme. Code starts closed to its first lines, with View code and Copy. The full states page is a link at the end.
- **Site only: the theme builder wears your theme.** As you pick, the whole page, its letter tiles, header, buttons and links, takes your color, faces and corners, in whichever theme it is showing.
- **A theme builder at `/make/`** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: "a tool that could be used by other people"). Pick a brand color and it builds the light and dark themes with Capsomer's own generator, in the browser: both 12-step scales, every text and control color, and all 136 pairs checked against WCAG 2.2, with a fix for each failure and a button that finds the nearest passing color. Faces (self-hosted), text size, corners and density; presets for the eight families and for Foxhound, Foxing, Julie Edwards and TXASM. Real components preview both themes side by side. It exports a CSS file in the `cap.tokens` layer, a W3C design tokens file and a link that reopens the theme. The front page and the header link to it.
- Prelum v0.14.0 for the site and tests.
- **Site only: specimens keep their inline styles.** The site's Content-Security-Policy allows no inline style, so every `style=""` in an `examples.html` was dropped on the states pages: the button's sweep, the range fill, the panel's lift and a few layout grids. `bin/states.mjs` now moves each one into the page's own `states.css`. Found by a Lighthouse run; the CSP browser test now checks a component page's states frames too.
- **Site only: a second pass over the public site** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "do another pass and examine website structure ... consider motion and interactive elements").
  - Every component has its own public page at `/components/<name>/`: its summary, its facts, every state in both themes and its whole doc, with Previous and Next. The front page's list links there; the list linked to a bare states page with no way back. The pages are in the sitemap.
  - On a phone the component list shows names in two columns (the front page went from 17,929 px tall to about 11,100).
  - The letter tiles rise out of their stripe: the cover's as the page opens, each section's as it scrolls into view (scroll-driven, so a browser without it shows them still). Nothing moves under reduced motion.
  - The family preview crossfades to the chosen family.
  - "Try your own words" sets every size in the type scale to your text. On a phone each size takes two lines, not four.
  - The footer's links sit in one row.
- **Site only:** `/.well-known/security.txt` (security@dustinedwards.info) and an IndexNow key file, with `.github/workflows/indexnow.yml` pinging IndexNow after each deploy. devkit's family check runs in CI (`.family.json` lists the old-spelling aliases it leaves alone).
- devkit v0.13.1, site-runtime v0.23.0 and Prelum v0.13.0 for the site and tests.
- **Site only: a critical pass over the public page and the admin** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "critically evaluate every aspect of the site"). Public page:
  - It uses Capsomer's own site header, with the theme button (and `T`), the phone sheet and a skip link. Before, it had a plain bar of its own and no theme control.
  - A live family preview: real components that take the chosen family through `data-family`.
  - A filter over the components. Nothing on the public page sends a visitor into the admin except the footer's Admin link.
  - Every default opens to its reason. The heading promised reasons the page did not show.
  - A copy button on the install lines.
  - The test count is read from the last run on main. The deployed build has no results of its own, so the figure was missing.
  - The heading says what Capsomer is, where it repeated the name the tiles already spell.
  - The admin is linked from the footer only, not the top menu, and the footer's links are just their names (Admin, Source, Changes).

  Admin:
  - Test results are filed under their component. Before, every test inside a shared `eachTheme` block counted as "site", so 1,711 of 1,819 landed there and most component pages said "No results".
  - Each component's tests fold away while they pass.
  - Changes shows unreleased work and the last three releases.
  - A long changelog line no longer pushes a phone page sideways.
  - The Settings page, which held two paragraphs and no settings, is gone; `#/settings` opens the overview.

## 0.26.2

- **The footer's Workspace link to the members' area reads "Lab bench"** (Dustin, 2026-10-26), not "Lab", since "Lab" names other parts of dustinedwards.info.
- **Capsomer's admin is named Scaffold** (Dustin, 2026-10-26), after the proteins that guide a capsid's assembly and leave before it is finished, as Capsid's is the Portal. In the admin shell's strip it is "Capsomer Scaffold" and goes to `/admin/` (it went to the public page). Site only: the admin's title, the footer link and llms.txt say Scaffold.

## 0.26.1

- **The family footer's strip is headed "Network"** (Dustin, 2026-10-26), not "The family". Changed HTML contract: its heading's id is `cap-family-footer-network` (was `cap-family-footer-family`).
- **The copyright sits right under Dustin Edwards** (Dustin, 2026-10-26), not below the tallest column. Changed HTML contract: `.cap-family-footer-note` moved into the name's column, beside the logo in a new wrapper `div`.

## 0.26.0

- **The family footer is now each site's one footer** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: option 4 of the footer mocks). `familyFooterHtml()` takes `site` (a `FAMILY_SITES` id) and `center` (the site's own HTML). Under a thin stripe in the page's color: the site's own part (`.cap-family-footer-center`, `-center-name`, `-about`, `-stamp`, with `.cap-family-footer-columns` on the letterhead's columns), then the strip of the family's sites (`nav.cap-family-footer-sites`), each marked in its family's color, with the one you are on carrying a heavy top edge. Then the double stripe over Dustin Edwards, as before. Changed HTML contract: the footer's markup now opens with `.cap-family-footer-local`.
- **`tokens/family-marks.css`**, in `tokens.css`: each family's color as `--family-<name>` (its `--on`, in both themes), so a page can mark the other families without loading their scopes. Generated by `tokens/palette.mjs` (`renderMarks`).
- **Site only:** the public pages' footer is the family footer with Capsomer's own part; the old letterhead and link row are gone.

## 0.25.0

- **The family footer** (`components/family-footer`, Dustin, 2026-10-26: "i want them to all have the same footer"). dustinedwards.info's footer, moved here so it and the public sites of Capsid and Capsomer all end with the same one, the last two under their own footer. `familyFooterHtml({ origin, published, year })` from `capsomer/behavior/family-footer` writes it; `capsomer/family-footer.css` styles it, with `manual.css` for its band and letterhead. Its band is the page family's `--on`, so each site draws it in its own color, and it leaves off the letterhead's heavy rule (Dustin: "remove that one black line"). The links are exported as data for a site's own tests. New classes only (`.cap-family-footer` and its parts); nothing is removed or renamed.
- **Site only:** the public pages end with the family footer, in magenta, under Capsomer's own.

## 0.24.0

- **The rest of NASA's marks in the manual theme** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: "Incorporate them all"), moved from Capsid's explorer so every public site shares one copy: the layout grid behind a page's head (`.cap-layout-grid`, twelve columns and a 24px baseline in faint red, four columns on a phone), the direction strip for a trail (`nav.cap-direction`, a dark band, arrows in the family's color, the current page lit), the livery band across the foot of a page and a card's stripe (`.cap-livery`, `[data-livery]`), the Saturn V roll blocks for steps (`ol.cap-roll`, `li[data-state="done|now|ahead"]`), the Apollo flight plan's time line (`ol.cap-timeline`, `.cap-timeline-mark`, `.cap-timeline-body`) and the Apollo cue card (`.cap-cue`, `.cap-cue-strip`, `.cap-cue-title`, `.cap-cue-lines`, `.cap-cue-name`, `.cap-cue-value`). New classes only; nothing is removed or renamed.
- **`family-purple.css`**: purple is now scoped on `[data-family="purple"]` too, like the other families, so a page whose root is another family can still show purple. `families.css` imports it first. Purple stays the default on `:root`.

## 0.23.0

- **The manual theme, `capsomer/manual.css`, for the public pages of the dustinedwards.info sites** (Dustin, 2026-10-26: "we are just theming our public pages"). The look of the 1976 NASA Graphics Standards Manual, lifted from Capsid's explorer so dustinedwards.info, Capsid's public site, Capsomer's public site and Prelum's specimen share one copy: a running head (and its sign variant), numbered parts with the title on the left, ruled form boxes (with readings and a tone scale), an index with dotted leaders, the checklist card, plates, signs for the ways on, the mission patch (`patchSvg()` from `capsomer/behavior/manual`) and the letterhead. New classes only: `cap-running-head`, `cap-part`, `cap-form-boxes`, `cap-leader-index`, `cap-checklist`, `cap-plate`, `cap-signs`, `cap-sign`, `cap-patch`, `cap-letterhead` and their parts. Opt-in: Capsomer's components, its admin and the products do not load it. The one color is the page family's `--on`.
- **Two new families, magenta and cerulean** (`data-family="magenta"`, `data-family="cerulean"`; `capsomer/family-magenta.css`, `capsomer/family-cerulean.css`, and both in `capsomer/families.css`). Purple's lightness and strength at hues no family had: magenta (seed #b24fac, hue 330) for Capsomer's public site, cerulean (seed #0588b4, hue 230) for Prelum's specimen. Every pair passes in both themes (1,088 pairs across eight families). `EmailFamily` gains both names.
- **The palette code runs in a browser.** The color math, the families and the renderers moved from `tokens/palette.mjs` into `tokens/palette-core.mjs` (in the package, with types), which has no file system use; `palette.mjs` re-exports it, so nothing that imports it changes. Two new exports, `seedLch` and `darkStep9`.
- **Any brand color makes a working theme.** A seed too dark for 3:1 on the dark surfaces (navy, black) is lightened for step 9 in dark, as amber is darkened in light (`darkStep9`), and lightness is held to 0 to 1, so a very light seed (yellow, white) no longer throws. No family's colors change.

## 0.22.0

**What the Capsid Portal and dustinedwards.info need to drop their own copies** (Dustin approved moving them onto Capsomer, 2026-10-26).

- **New: `ReasonForm`** in `capsomer/react/switch-reason`: the one-line reason on its own, for a change that is not a switch (the Portal's "Runs on" radio group). Props: `open`, `verb`, `onApply(reason)` (a rejection keeps it open with the error), `onCancel`, and optional `failed`, `id`, `placeholder` and `onPendingChange`. `SwitchWithReason` is now built on it; its markup is unchanged.
- **New: `SwitchWithReason`'s `id` prop**: the switch input's id, so an app can focus it again after an Undo or press it from a command. The reason field is then `<id>-why`.
- **New: the theme switch's no-script form**, `data-variant="form"` (React `ThemeForm`): two submit buttons posting to the site's theme route, one shown by CSS, for a site that keeps the choice in a cookie. `enhance()` makes a press switch at once and write the cookie (`data-cap-cookie`). New classes: `.cap-theme-form`. dustinedwards.info's own theme toggle moves onto it.
- **New: `ConfirmModal`**, the confirm page drawn over the page as a modal that a server opens, in the page until script runs (`.cap-confirm-modal`, `data-inline`). `ConfirmPage` gains `children`, `onCancel` and `renderForm`, its `action` may be left out (it posts to the page it is on), and a `lead` that is not a string is drawn in a `div`. dustinedwards.info's own admin confirm dialog moves onto it.
- **`Button` takes a `ref`** in its props type, so an app can move focus to it (the Portal's confirm dialog focuses Cancel or the action after a preview). React 19 already passed it through; only the type is new.
- **The chart palette's color difference is Enarratio's.** `tokens/chart.mjs` kept its own copy of CIEDE2000 and the color-vision simulation. The checks now live in `tokens/chart-checks.mjs`, which uses Enarratio's `colorDifference` and is not in the package. `colorDifference`, `worstDifference`, `VISIONS`, `chartChecks` and the three thresholds are no longer exported from `tokens/chart.mjs`; no app imported them. `check()` in `tokens/palette.mjs` is now async.

## 0.21.0

**Shared server code, moved here from the sites** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "we keep finding things that are not moved to the shared core components").

- **New: the theme switch's server half, `capsomer/theme-server`**: `readThemeCookie`, `themeCookie`, `colorSchemeContent` and `themeScript`, with no DOM, so a server-rendered site writes the person's theme into the first byte. dustinedwards.info, Foxhound and txasm each wrote their own; they move onto this. `THEME_PREF` and `ThemeChoice` now live there; `capsomer/behavior/theme-switch` still exports both.
- **One HTML escape inside Capsomer.** `chart-frame`, `system-map` and `draft-compare` now use the email builder's `escapeHtml` (they still export it under the same names). system-map and draft-compare now also escape `'`, which is safe and changes no visible text.

- **American spelling throughout** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "i literally live in Texas. why the fuck would i want british spelling on anything"). Every doc, comment, test name and the showcase now spell color, behavior, center, gray, labeled, neighbor, catalog, license, summarize and analyze the American way. Nothing that apps use breaks; each renamed name keeps its old spelling working:
  - Import path `capsomer/behavior/<name>`; `capsomer/behaviour/<name>` still resolves.
  - `summarize` (authorship), `analyze` (draft compare) and `renderEmailColors` (palette); `summarise`, `analyse` and `renderEmailColours` stay as deprecated aliases.
  - Dialog `placement="center"`; `"centre"` still type-checks (both are the default, with no CSS of their own).
  - Custom properties `--cap-meter-color` and `--cap-run-color` (authorship); each still reads `--cap-meter-colour` / `--cap-run-colour` when an app sets it.
  - Renamed outright, with no alias: the `tokens.json` groups `colour-light` and `colour-dark` (now `color-light`, `color-dark`; no app found reading them), the internal `tokens/color.css` (was `colour.css`), and the test-name prefix `behavior:`. `aria-labelledby` and the React prop `labelledBy` keep the spelling the ARIA standard uses.
- **Site only: a public front page, and the dashboard moves to `/admin/`** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "we need a capsomer admin ... and a capsomer public display"). https://capsomer.dustinedwards.info is now a plain HTML page in the Supergraphics style (Barbara Stauffacher Solomon, The Sea Ranch, 1966): each section opens with its name in flat letter tiles painted in the families, too big for the page and cut by its edge, then the six families with their twelve steps, the three faces and the type scale, every component linked to its states page, the defaults and how to install. Its only script sends an old root address such as `/#/components/button` to the same page under `/admin/`. The admin is unchanged at https://capsomer.dustinedwards.info/admin/, with its own title from `site/seo.ts` and noindex, so search shows the public page; it stays out of the sitemap and is listed in llms.txt. The package is unchanged.
- **The showcase site's search and AI surface now comes from Prelum** (`@drdustinedwards/site-helpers` 0.8.0, a dev dependency, site tooling only), as on every family site: the front page's canonical, Open Graph and Twitter tags with a share card (`site/public/og-card.png`) and JSON-LD; the sitemap, robots.txt (the family bot policy), a new ai.txt and llms.txt, written at build from `site/seo.ts`; and `tdm-reservation: 1` in `_headers`. The 404 page says noindex. The package itself is unchanged.

## 0.20.0

**Foxhound's two asks before launch** (capsomer job_adef3dadfa88, for foxhound job_490a6bc5b957 and job_69b3fb23214b).

- **AdminShell can leave the tab title to the app.** New prop `documentTitle` (default `true`, today's "<page> · <app>"). With `documentTitle={false}` the shell never writes `document.title`, so the titles an app's routes set stay; `title` still names the logo, the phone sheet and its groups. New specimen `own-title`. The default is unchanged.
- **New stylesheet: `capsomer/utilities.css`**, Capsomer's utility classes alone (`.cap-sr-only`, `.cap-mono`, `.cap-num`, `.cap-muted`, `.cap-label`), in the `cap.utilities` layer, for an app that keeps its own reset and body styles and does not load `capsomer/base.css`. `css/base.css` now imports it, so an app that loads base.css gets the same rules as before. No class is renamed or removed.

## 0.19.0

**Foxhound's public pages, as Recova's** (Dustin, 2026-10-25: "there is still so much wrong with foxhound... as compared to recova", and "dont forget the ui/ux, and hover and active etc").

- **New: a panel that is a link** (`a.cap-panel`). Under the pointer its edge firms to `--line-strong`; its words keep the text color. New HTML contract only; a panel that is not a link is unchanged.
- **New touch tokens**, group `touch`, each off by default so no site changes unless its `SITES` entry sets them: `--card-lift` (`0px`; how far a linked panel rises under the pointer), `--card-edge` (`0`; its edge under the pointer, from the strong line at 0 to the accent at 1), `--card-shadow` (`none`; every panel's resting shadow) and `--btn-sweep` (`0`; at 1 the default button fills with the primary color from the left under the pointer, its label and edge turning to match).
- **New: the disclosure's card variant** (`data-variant="card"`): each section its own rounded card, a roomy title at the lead size with a plus at the end that turns to a cross when open, no rule under the title. Recova's questions list.
- **New: the segmented control's cards layout** (`data-layout="cards"` on `.cap-seg-options`; React `layout="cards"`): each option a bordered card, two to a row and four from 640px, left aligned and wrapping. `data-tone="info|warn|crit|ok"` on an option (React `tone`) tints it in that status color when chosen; the accent otherwise. The chosen highlight does not slide in this layout. Recova's decline picker.
- **New: a slider** (`input.cap-range`, in `capsomer/field.css`; React `Range` from the field module): a native range input on a 4px track filled with the primary color up to the thumb (`--cap-range-fill`), a 20px thumb with a hover ring, the focus ring and a small press.
